\displaystyle\equiv\left(\lambda z.\mathcal{J}[a(i)/x][b(i)/y][\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]\right)(c)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathcal{J}[a(i)/x][b(i)/y][\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}][c/z]

We have arranged the syntax of our admissible rules so that function
definitions and uses click together, as in ordinary function
evaluation: comparing
f(a(i),b(i),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.,c)
and

f(x,y,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},z)
, the first arguments become substitutions

[a(i)/x][b(i)/y]
and the amazing
\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.
‘argument’ becomes a
‘substitution’
[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]
.

Any ordinary function
f:A\to B
is tautologically equal to the definition

\displaystyle f(x):\equiv f(x)

by
\eta
-expansion. For
\surd
, this
\eta
-expansion introduces
an instance of the counit. That is, any term
r:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}A
is
tautologically equal to the definition

\displaystyle r(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}):\equiv r[i/{\mathcal{L}}](\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)

Remark 3.5
.

Using copattern syntax could also apply to other right adjoint
modalities, like
\sharp

[
Shu18
]
. In the
original syntax, functoriality of
\sharp
on a function

f:A\to B
is defined by

\sharp f(u):\equiv f(u_{\sharp})^{\sharp}
. In a copattern-style
syntax, this might be written

\sharp f(u,\sharp):\equiv f(u(\sharp))
, or

\sharp f(u,\sharp_{\mathsf{i}}):\equiv f(u(\sharp_{\mathsf{e}}))

if we wish to distinguish the symbols for introduction and
elimination. Here, the interpretation is that a variable placed to
the left of
\sharp_{\mathsf{i}}
becomes crisp in the body of the function, and
an ‘application’
s(\sharp_{\mathsf{e}})
is only well-typed when
s
is crisp.
In this case,
u
becomes crisp and so it is valid to apply it to

\sharp_{\mathsf{e}}
.

Second:

We will often need to extract terms of
A
from
assumptions
r:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}A
using the extract map
\mathsf{e}_{A}

defined above. When such assumptions are behind many context locks,
the
\surd
-elim
rule provides a term
i:\mathbb{T}
that can
be used to unlock all these locks at once, giving us access to
A

regardless of where
r
lies in the context.

Definition 3.6
.

If a variable
x:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}A
is behind several locks

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{1}}\dots\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}_{n}}
, then let

\displaystyle{x}_{\downharpoonleft}:\equiv(x\llbracket i/{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\dots,i/{\mathcal{K}_{n}}\rrbracket)(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.):A[i/{\mathcal{K}_{1}},\dots,i/{\mathcal{K}_{n}}][\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]

in particular, if
x:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}A
is behind no locks in the
context then
{x}_{\downharpoonleft}:\equiv\mathsf{e}_{A}(x)
.

This notation
{x}_{\downharpoonleft}
is intended to evoke the elimination rule

x_{\sharp}
used in cohesive HoTT (and similar). It cannot be
internalised as a function, it is just an admissible piece of syntax.

Using these shorthands, the definition of functoriality is much less
noisy: For
A,B:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
and

f:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left({A}_{\downharpoonleft}\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)
we have

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}f:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft}\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}{B}_{\downharpoonleft}

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}f(r,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}):\equiv{f}_{\downharpoonleft}({r}_{\downharpoonleft})

The comultiplication of
\surd
can be also written nicely using
this notation. For a type
A:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
, define

\displaystyle\delta_{A}:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft}\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft}

\displaystyle\delta_{A}(r,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}):\equiv{r}_{\downharpoonleft}

We can show that, as a right adjoint,
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{}}$}}
preserves
1
and pullbacks.

Proposition 3.7
.

\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}1\simeq 1

Proof.

Any term
r:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}1
will
\eta
-expand as

r\equiv\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}.r\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)\equiv\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}.\star
and so
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}1
is contractible.
∎

Proposition 3.8
.

If
A:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
and

B:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}({A}_{\downharpoonleft}\to\mathcal{U})
there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}}{\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{B}_{\downharpoonleft}(x)\right)\simeq\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(x:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.59117pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}}{\sum_{(x:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.59117pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(x:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.59117pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(x:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.59117pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}({B}_{\downharpoonleft}({x}_{\downharpoonleft}))

Proof.

Define maps either way by

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}.\mathsf{pr}_{1}{r}_{\downharpoonleft},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.\mathsf{pr}_{2}{r}_{\downharpoonleft})

\displaystyle(s,t)

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}.({s}_{\downharpoonleft},{t}_{\downharpoonleft})

∎

Proposition 3.9
.

For any
A:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
and
r,s:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft}
, there
is an equivalence

\displaystyle(r=s)\simeq\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\left({r}_{\downharpoonleft}={s}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

Proof.

By the fundamental theorem of
\mathsf{Id}
-types, we just have to check
that for fixed
r:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft}
, the type

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(s:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}}{\sum_{(s:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(s:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(s:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\left({r}_{\downharpoonleft}={s}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

is contractible. By
Proposition

3.8
, this
type is equivalent to

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}}{\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{\sum_{(x:{A}_{\downharpoonleft})}}{r}_{\downharpoonleft}=x\right)

and the interior is a contractible pair.
∎

This is more recognisable as a statement of left-exactness with the
left side
\eta
-expanded:

Corollary 3.10
.

(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}.{r}_{\downharpoonleft}=\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}.{s}_{\downharpoonleft})\simeq\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left({r}_{\downharpoonleft}={s}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

3.2
Adjointness

We now investigate how
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}
relates to
(\mathbb{T}\to-)
. It is
well-known that, although
(\mathbb{T}\to-)
externally has a right
adjoint, it is not typically a
fibred
right adjoint unless

\mathbb{T}\simeq 1

[
Yet87
,
Koc05
]
.

There is, however, a useful internal statement. To begin, unit and
counit maps for the adjunction.

Definition 3.11
.

If
A,B:\mathcal{U}
are (for now) closed types, there are maps

\displaystyle\eta_{A}

\displaystyle:A\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}(\mathbb{T}\to A)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{B}

\displaystyle:(\mathbb{T}\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}B)\to B

given by

\displaystyle\eta_{A}(a,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},t)

\displaystyle:\equiv a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{B}(f)

\displaystyle:\equiv f(i,\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)

Allowing the maximum amount of dependency, for
any

A:\mathcal{U}
, there is a map

\displaystyle\eta_{A}

\displaystyle:A\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket

and for
any

B:\mathbb{T}\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
a map

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{B}

\displaystyle:\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}{B(t)}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)\to B(j,\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j.)

with the same definitions as in the closed case.

Besides
\varepsilon_{B}
not being definable for an arbitrary type
B
,
we have also seen that
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}-
is not functorial on arbitrary maps

A\to B
. So to what extent is
(\mathbb{T}\to-)\dashv\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}-
? We
can prove an internal adjointness statement analogous to one
in
[
FHM03
, §3]
for adjunctions in which the
left adjoint is strong monoidal.

Proposition 3.12
.

If
A
and
B
are closed types then there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\Phi:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}((\mathbb{T}\to A)\to B)\to(A\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}B)

Happily, we can allow
A
and
B
to be dependent types:

Proposition 3.13
.

For any
A:\mathcal{U}
and
B:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
, there is an equivalence

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}((\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft})\to(A\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}{B}_{\downharpoonleft})

And in fact, we can also let
B
depend on
A
. In full generality:

Proposition 3.14
.

For any
A:\mathcal{U}
and
B:A\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
, there is an
equivalence

\displaystyle\Phi:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(h:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(h:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(h:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(h:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}B\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(h(i),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)\right)\to\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(a:A)}}}{\prod_{(a:A)}}{\prod_{(a:A)}}{\prod_{(a:A)}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}{B(a)}_{\downharpoonleft}

given by

\displaystyle\Phi(r,a,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}})

\displaystyle:\equiv r\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.,\lambda t.a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}(f,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}},h)

\displaystyle:\equiv f\llbracket k/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket(h(k),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k.)

Proof.

All types involved have definitional
\eta
-principles, so this is a
matter of crunching through the composites in both directions.

First, we
\eta
-expand
\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(r))
to
\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{J}}.\lambda h.\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(r\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j.,h)
and simplify the body:

\displaystyle\Phi^{-1}(\Phi(r\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j.,h)

\displaystyle\equiv\Phi(r\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket)[k/{\mathcal{K}}][\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}](h(k),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k.)

(Definition of
\Phi^{-1}
)

\displaystyle\equiv\Phi(r\llbracket k/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket)(h(k),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k.)

(Computing
[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}]
)

\displaystyle\equiv r\llbracket k/{\mathcal{K}},i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.,\lambda t.h(k)\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]

(Definition of
\Phi
)

\displaystyle\equiv r\llbracket i/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.,\lambda t.h(t))

(Computing
[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]
)

\displaystyle\equiv r\llbracket i/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.,h)

(
\eta
for functions)

which is precisely the
\eta
-expansion of
r
.

The other way,
\Phi(\Phi^{-1}(f))
expands to
\lambda a.\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{J}}.\Phi(\Phi^{-1}(f\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket,a,\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j.))
and:

\displaystyle\Phi(\Phi^{-1}(f\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket),a\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket,\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j.)

\displaystyle\equiv\Phi^{-1}(f\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket)\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.,\lambda t.a\llbracket j/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]

(Definition of
\Phi
)

\displaystyle\equiv\Phi^{-1}(f\llbracket i/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket)(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.,\lambda t.a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket)

(Computing
[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}j./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]
)

\displaystyle\equiv f\llbracket i/{\mathcal{J}},k/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}]((\lambda t.a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket)(k),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k.)

(Definition of
\Phi^{-1}
)

\displaystyle\equiv f\llbracket k/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket((\lambda t.a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket)(k),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k.)

(Computing
[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}]
)

\displaystyle\equiv f\llbracket k/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket(a\llbracket k/{\mathcal{J}}\rrbracket,\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}k.)

(
\beta
for functions)

is the
\eta
-expansion of
f
.

∎

Remark 3.15
.

Internal equivalences of this kind for an arbitrary adjoint modality
have been defined in MTT, where the modality is instead a positive
type-former, see
[
ND21
, Proposition 3.4]

and
[
GKNB20
, Section 10.4]
. The fact that this internal
formulation circumvents the ‘no-go’ theorem of
[
LOPS18
]
was also
noted in
[
ND21
, Section 10.1]
.

4
Constructions

4.1
Higher-Dimensional Induction

Since we have given
\mathbb{T}\to-
a right adjoint, it now preserves
colimits. In particular, we support the same kind of
“higher-dimensional pattern matching” on functions out of
\mathbb{T}
,
as suggested in
[
ND21
, §2.4]
. In this section we derive
an induction principle for functions of
\mathbb{T}
into coproducts,
thought we expect easy generalisations to other inductive types.

Proposition 4.1
.

Suppose types
A,B:\mathcal{U}
and a type family

P:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket\right)\to\mathcal{U}\right).

Then there is a map

\displaystyle\mathsf{hind}:

\displaystyle\,\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(\mathsf{inl}\circ l)\right)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\,\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(\mathsf{inr}\circ r)\right)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\,\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(f)\right)

such that
\mathsf{hind}(g,h)
computes via

\displaystyle\mathsf{hind}(g,h)(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)))

\displaystyle\equiv{g}_{\downharpoonleft}({l}_{\downharpoonleft})

\displaystyle\mathsf{hind}(g,h)(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inr}({r}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)))

\displaystyle\equiv{h}_{\downharpoonleft}({r}_{\downharpoonleft})

for any
l:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:T)}}}{\prod_{(t:T)}}{\prod_{(t:T)}}{\prod_{(t:T)}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)
and
r:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:T)}}}{\prod_{(t:T)}}{\prod_{(t:T)}}{\prod_{(t:T)}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)
.

If we like, we can always extract the underlying function

\mathsf{hind}(g,h)(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(f:\mathbb{T}\to A+B)}}}{\prod_{(f:\mathbb{T}\to A+B)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathbb{T}\to A+B)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathbb{T}\to A+B)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(f)
of
the result.

Proof.

There is a chain of equivalences

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(l:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(\mathsf{inl}\circ l)\right)\times\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(r:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(\mathsf{inr}\circ r)\right)

(Dependent adjointness)

\displaystyle\simeq\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(a:A)}}}{\prod_{(a:A)}}{\prod_{(a:A)}}{\prod_{(a:A)}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda i.\mathsf{inl}(a\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket))\right)\times\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(b:B)}}}{\prod_{(b:B)}}{\prod_{(b:B)}}{\prod_{(b:B)}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda i.\mathsf{inr}(b\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket))\right)

(Universal property of
+
)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:A+B)}}}{\prod_{(s:A+B)}}{\prod_{(s:A+B)}}{\prod_{(s:A+B)}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda i.s\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)

(Dependent adjointness)

\displaystyle\simeq\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(f)\right)

As a term, the forward map is

\displaystyle\mathsf{hind}(g,h)(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},f):\equiv\mathsf{case}_{+}(f(i),

\displaystyle a.\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.{g}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda t.a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket),

\displaystyle b.\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.{h}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda t.b\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket))(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)

We can show directly that this map has the desired computational properties:

\displaystyle\mathsf{hind}(g,h)(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)))

\displaystyle\equiv\mathsf{case}_{+}(\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(i)),a.\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.{g}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda t.a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket),b.\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.{h}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda t.b\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket))(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)

\displaystyle\equiv(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.{g}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda t.{l}_{\downharpoonleft}\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket(i\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket)))(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)

\displaystyle\equiv{g}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda t.{l}_{\downharpoonleft}\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket(i\llbracket t/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket))[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}]

\displaystyle\equiv{g}_{\downharpoonleft}(\lambda t.{l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t))

\displaystyle\equiv{g}_{\downharpoonleft}({l}_{\downharpoonleft})

and similarly when evaluated at
\lambda t.\mathsf{inr}({r}_{\downharpoonleft}(t))
.
∎

The upshot of the above is that we support higher-dimension pattern
matching of the following form: to define a function

p:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)}}{P}_{\downharpoonleft}(f)\right)
, it suffices to give cases

\displaystyle p(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)))

\displaystyle:\equiv\dots

\displaystyle p(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inr}({r}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)))

\displaystyle:\equiv\dots

An immediate application is:

Proposition 4.2
.

For any types
A,B:\mathcal{U}
, the map

\displaystyle\mathsf{split}_{A+B}:\left((\mathbb{T}\to A)+(\mathbb{T}\to B)\right)\to(\mathbb{T}\to A+B)

defined by case-splitting is an equivalence.

Proof.

The inverse map is definable using this new pattern matching, with
motive the constant type family

\displaystyle P(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},i):\equiv\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket\right)+\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket\right)

The cases we have to provide are:

\displaystyle\mathsf{unsplit}_{A+B}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)))

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft})

\displaystyle\mathsf{unsplit}_{A+B}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inr}({r}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)))

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{inr}({r}_{\downharpoonleft})

In one direction, the composite computes definitionally:

\displaystyle\mathsf{unsplit}_{A+B}(\mathsf{split}_{A+B}(\mathsf{inl}(f)))\equiv\mathsf{unsplit}_{A+B}(\lambda i.\mathsf{inl}(f(i)))\equiv\mathsf{inl}(f)

In the other direction, we use higher-dimensional pattern matching again, this time with motive

\displaystyle P^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},f):\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}\mathsf{split}_{A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket}\left(\mathsf{unsplit}_{A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket}(f)\right)(t)=f(t)

In the branches, the left-hand side of the path computes away:

\displaystyle\mathsf{split}(\mathsf{unsplit}(\lambda t.\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t))))\equiv\mathsf{split}(\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}))\equiv\lambda t.\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t))

and similarly for
\mathsf{inr}
, so we may inhabit the type family by

\displaystyle\mathsf{inv}_{A+B}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)),t)

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{refl}_{\mathsf{inl}({l}_{\downharpoonleft}(t))}

\displaystyle\mathsf{inv}_{A+B}(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},\lambda t.\mathsf{inr}({r}_{\downharpoonleft}(t)),t)

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathsf{refl}_{\mathsf{inr}({r}_{\downharpoonleft}(t))}

proving (via function extensionality) that
\mathsf{unsplit}_{A+B}
is
indeed an inverse.
∎

Notice that we did not need the input types
A
and
B
to be guarded by

\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}
in the above Proposition. With a little more care, we could
obtain a more refined map

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\left((\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)+(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)\right)\to(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket+B\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)\right)

whence the original equivalence may be extracted.

4.2
Transpension

An equivalent characterisation of a tiny object in a 1-topos, due to
Freyd
[
Yet87
, Proposition 1.2]
, is an object
\mathbb{T}
such
that the dependent product functor

\Pi_{\mathbb{T}}:\mathcal{E}/\mathbb{T}\to\mathcal{E}
has a right adjoint

\nabla_{\mathbb{T}}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{E}/\mathbb{T}
. This operation was
given the name
transpension
in
[
ND20
,
ND21
]
.

If
\mathbb{T}
is tiny in the ordinary sense, then in a 1-topos this
adjoint
\nabla_{\mathbb{T}}
can be defined by sending each object
B
to
the left map in the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bullet}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(P:\Omega)}}}{\sum_{(P:\Omega)}}{\sum_{(P:\Omega)}}{\sum_{(P:\Omega)}}P\to B\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\Omega}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nabla_{\mathbb{T}}B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\scriptstyle\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathsf{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\chi_{\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{T}}})^{\vee}}

In the univalent setting this construction is no longer correct, but
simply replacing
\Omega
with the universe works: for

B:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
, define the type family

{B}^{1/-}:\mathbb{T}\to\mathcal{U}
by the pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{B}^{1/t}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(X:\mathcal{U})}}}{\sum_{(X:\mathcal{U})}}{\sum_{(X:\mathcal{U})}}{\sum_{(X:\mathcal{U})}}X\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\scriptstyle\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathsf{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{T}})^{\vee}}

where the map along the bottom is the transpose of

(\lambda f.f=\mathsf{id}_{\mathbb{T}}):(\mathbb{T}\to\mathbb{T})\to\mathcal{U}
, namely

\displaystyle t\mapsto\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}t\llbracket s/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket=s

We can simplify this description a fair amount. Because
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}

commutes with
\Sigma
-types, the map on the right is equivalent to
the projection

\displaystyle\mathsf{pr}_{1}:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(X:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U})}}}{\sum_{(X:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U})}}{\sum_{(X:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U})}}{\sum_{(X:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U})}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}({X}_{\downharpoonleft}\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft})\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}

The fibre of the pullback over a fixed
t:\mathbb{T}
is then easy to
calculate: it is equivalent to the type of the second component of the sum:

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}({X}_{\downharpoonleft}\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft})[(\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}t\llbracket s/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket=s)/X]

\displaystyle\equiv\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left((\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}t\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket=s)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

and so we take this as our definition.

Definition 4.3
.

For
B:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}
, define the
transpension

B^{1/-}:\mathbb{T}\to\mathcal{U}
to be

\displaystyle{B}^{1/t}:\equiv\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left((\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}t\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket=s)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

The whole point of this construction was to be a right adjoint to the dependent product with
\mathbb{T}
, and indeed it is:

Proposition 4.4
.

For
A:\mathbb{T}\to\mathcal{U}
and
B:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(s)\right)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)\simeq\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t)\to{B}^{1/t}\right)

Proof.

To apply dependent adjointness (
Proposition

3.14
) we
need to massage the interior of the
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}
a little. Let
P

denote the type family

\displaystyle P:\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t)\right)\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle P((t,a),\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}):\equiv(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}t\llbracket s/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket t=s)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}

Then

\displaystyle\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}(A\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)(s)\right)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(a:A\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}}{\sum_{(a:A\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}{\sum_{(a:A\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}{\sum_{(a:A\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}(t=s)\right)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

(Contractible pair)

\displaystyle\simeq\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}\mathsf{pr}_{1}(f(i))=s\right)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

(Univ. property of
\Sigma
)

\displaystyle\equiv\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}{\prod_{(f:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t^{\prime}:\mathbb{T})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A\llbracket t^{\prime}/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(t))}}P\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(f(i),\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)\right)

(Definition of
P
)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}(P\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)(t\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket,a\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket,\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.)

(
Proposition

3.14
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left((\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(s:\mathbb{T})}}t\llbracket s/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket=s)\to{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\right)

(Definition of
P
)

\displaystyle\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}{\prod_{((t,a):\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\sum_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t))}}{B}^{1/t}

(Definition of
B^{1/t}
)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}A(t)\to{B}^{1/t}

(Currying)

∎

Corollary 4.5
.

\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.71562pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{B}_{\downharpoonleft}\simeq\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(t:\mathbb{T})}}{B}^{1/t}\right)

Proof.

Plug in
A(t):\equiv 1
in the above.
∎

A surprising fact is that the pullback of
\mathbb{T}
to any slice

\mathcal{E}/X
is itself also tiny
[
Yet87
, Theorem 1.4]
,
but crucially, the right adjoint witnessing the tininess of

\mathbb{T}\times X
in
\mathcal{E}/X
is not simply the pullback of

\surd
.

By a similar strategy as for transpension, we can describe the correct
right adjoint internally. Given a family
D:X\to\mathcal{U}
over a
closed type
X
, this right adjoint

\surd_{\mkern-6.0mu/-}D:X\to\mathcal{U}
(to coin some notation) may be
calculated by

\surd_{\mkern-6.0mu/x}D:\equiv\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(x^{\prime}:X)}}}{\prod_{(x^{\prime}:X)}}{\prod_{(x^{\prime}:X)}}{\prod_{(x^{\prime}:X)}}\left(\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(k:\mathbb{T})}}}{\prod_{(k:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(k:\mathbb{T})}}{\prod_{(k:\mathbb{T})}}x^{\prime}=x\llbracket k/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket\right)\to D(x^{\prime})\right)

We have not yet encountered a use for this construction.

5
Applications

We end with some commentary on possible applications of our theory.

Synthetic Differential Geometry.

In future work with David
Jaz Myers, we intend to construct form classifiers of various kinds
internal to type theory and prove the properties asserted
axiomatically in
[
Mye21
, §3]
and
[
Mye22
, Appendix
A]
. In particular, we plan to give a synthetic
construction of the de Rham complex by constructing a long exact sequence
of form classifiers
\mathbb{R}\to\Lambda^{1}\to\Lambda^{2}\to\cdots
.

In this setting there are many important tiny objects, for example,
not only is the infinitesimal interval
D
tiny, but also each
“infinitesimal patch” around the origin in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
:

\displaystyle D(n):\equiv\{x:\mathbb{R}^{n}\mid\forall ij\,\,x_{i}x_{j}=0\}

Our new judgemental rules and type former have a straightforward
extension to multiple tiny objects, with each a closed type. Each

\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{}}
and
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}
would be annotated with the corresponding
tiny object, and the rules for the
\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}
-type would be
specialised for each tiny object.

We take this opportunity to propose some notation to be used when
there are multiple tiny objects at hand. On account of the copattern
notation, we suggest writing the amazing right adjoint as

\mathbb{T}\looparrowright A
rather than
{}^{\mathbb{T}}\mkern-12.0mu\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}A

or similar. This would associate to the right like ordinary function
types so that, for example, the unit of the adjunction would be
written

\displaystyle\eta_{A}:A\to\mathbb{T}\looparrowright\mathbb{T}\to A

\displaystyle\eta_{A}(a,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},t):\equiv a\llbracket t/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket

with the argument list aligning nicely with the structure of the type.

Internal Models of Cubical Type Theory.

The present type
theory removes the necessity for axioms in the construction of a
universe of fibrations in
[
LOPS18
, Theorem 5.2]
. Let us briefly
repeat the construction to demonstrate where things become more
explicit.

The ambient type theory is intensional, predicative MLTT with function
extensionality and UIP: we therefore denote the ambient universe

\mathsf{Set}
rather than
\mathcal{U}
and aim to construct a new universe

\mathsf{U}
that classifies fibrations. There is an interval type

\mathbb{I}
which is assumed to be tiny.

The main input data is a
notion of composition structure
, which
is a function
\mathsf{C}:(\mathbb{I}\to\mathsf{Set})\to\mathsf{Set}
that parameterises
the notion of fibration:

\displaystyle\isFib:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}}{\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}}{\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}\mathsf{Set}

\displaystyle\isFib(\Gamma)(A):\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(p:\mathbb{I}\to\Gamma)}}}{\prod_{(p:\mathbb{I}\to\Gamma)}}{\prod_{(p:\mathbb{I}\to\Gamma)}}{\prod_{(p:\mathbb{I}\to\Gamma)}}\mathsf{C}(A\circ p)

\displaystyle\Fib:\mathsf{Set}\to\mathsf{Set}

\displaystyle\Fib(\Gamma):\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}}{\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}\isFib(\Gamma)(A)

The construction proceeds the same way as in
[
LOPS18
, Theorem 5.2]
,
with the universe classifying ‘crisp’ fibrations constructed as the
pullback

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(A:\mathsf{Set})}}}{\sum_{(A:\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(A:\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(A:\mathsf{Set})}}A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{Set}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathsf{Set}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lrcorner}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.5581pt}{$\scriptstyle\SavedStyle\surd$}}\mathsf{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{C}^{\vee}}

We can simplify this description by commuting
\surd
with

\Sigma
and substituting, a move not available when working with
the axiomatically asserted tininess. A more convenient definition of

\mathsf{U}
is then

\displaystyle\mathsf{U}

\displaystyle:\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}}{\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}{A}_{\downharpoonleft}[\mathsf{C}^{\vee}(X)/A]

\displaystyle\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}}{\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(X:\mathsf{Set})}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\mathsf{C}(\lambda j.X\llbracket j/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket)

But inspecting this construction, things are much simpler if we
slightly change our notion of fibration. Surrounding
\mathsf{C}
with

\surd
in the body of
\isFib
, define:

\displaystyle\isNewFib:\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}}{\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(\Gamma:\mathsf{Set})}}\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}}{\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\prod_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}\mathsf{Set}

\displaystyle\isNewFib(\Gamma)(A):\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(g:\Gamma)}}}{\prod_{(g:\Gamma)}}{\prod_{(g:\Gamma)}}{\prod_{(g:\Gamma)}}\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{L}}$}}\mathsf{C}(\lambda i.A\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket(g\llbracket i/{\mathcal{L}}\rrbracket))

\displaystyle\NewFib:\mathsf{Set}\to\mathsf{Set}

\displaystyle\NewFib(\Gamma):\equiv\mathchoice{{\textstyle\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}}{\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}{\sum_{(A:\Gamma\to\mathsf{Set})}}\isNewFib(\Gamma)(A)

Unchanged, the type
\mathsf{U}
defined above classifies

non-crisp
fibrations of this tweaked kind, in that there is a
completely unrestricted equivalence between the type of functions

\Gamma\to\mathsf{U}
and
\NewFib(\Gamma)
.
We can construct an element of
\NewFib\mathsf{U}
tautologically,
running the identity
\mathsf{U}\to\mathsf{U}
backwards through that
equivalence. The proofs of fibrancy for each type constructor would
need to be re-done to account for the presence of
\surd
in

\isNewFib
, something we also leave for future work. There is the
intriguing prospect of these proofs amounting to a constructive

implementation
of cubical type theory internal to MLTT with a
tiny object, if enough of the non-computational axioms used
in
[
LOPS18
]
can be avoided.

Higher-Order Abstract Syntax.

I am indebted to Jon Sterling for pointing out the following
application. In
[
FPT99
,
FT01
]
, the
authors work in the internal language of the category of functors

\mathsf{FinSet}\to\mathsf{Set}
to give semantics for higher-order abstract
syntax. The inclusion
\mathbb{V}:\mathsf{FinSet}\to\mathsf{Set}
is thought of
as an object of
abstract variables
. It is tiny, because it is
represented by
1^{\mathsf{op}}\in\mathsf{FinSet}^{\mathsf{op}}
.

Suppose we have an inductive type
\mathtt{Tm}
representing the abstract
syntax of the untyped
\lambda
-calculus, and want to implement
single-variable substitution

\mathsf{subst}:(\mathbb{V}\to\mathtt{Tm})\to\mathtt{Tm}\to\mathtt{Tm}
so that

\mathsf{subst}(\lambda i.v,u)
substitutes
u
for
i
wherever it occurs
in
v
. We need to induct on
v
“under the binder” for
i
, and the
tininess of
\mathbb{V}
allows us to do this by similar techniques
to
Section

4.1
. Applying adjointness, it will be enough to
define the conjugate operation with type

\mathtt{Tm}\to\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd$}}(\mathtt{Tm}\to\mathtt{Tm})
, which can be done by ordinary
induction on
\mathtt{Tm}
.

Implementation.

Finally, we have a prototype
implementation of a type-checker available at

https://github.com/mvr/tiny
, using a novel extension of
normalisation-by-evaluation to handle the new context structure and
type former. The key insight is that the semantic environment
corresponding to a context
\Gamma,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}
can be a

meta-level function
from semantic values of type
\mathbb{T}
to semantic
environments for the context
\Gamma
. When looking up a variable in
the environment, the values attached to the variable as keys are used
as the arguments to these functions. Our type-checker verifies that
the two round-trips in
Proposition

3.14
are indeed proven by

\mathsf{refl}
in both directions
(
https://github.com/mvr/tiny/blob/master/adjunction.tiny
). The
normalisation algorithm works in practice, but it is left for future
work to verify that it works in theory.

Appendix A
Proofs

In this appendix, we provide proofs that the admissible rules described in

Section

2
preserve typing.

Establishing metatheoretic properties of our type theory is somewhat
easier than modal theories like, for example,
[
GSB19
]

and
[
RFL21
]
, because the new rules only ever extend the
context on the right rather than performing some manipulation
potentially at any point in the middle. In particular, it is not
necessary to prove versions of ‘lock strengthening/weakening/exchange’
as in
[
GSB19
]
, or ‘marking/unmarking’ operations as in

[
RFL21
]
.

Throughout, we continue to let
\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}
stand in for a sequence such
as
t_{1}/{\mathcal{L}_{1}},\dots,t_{n}/{\mathcal{L}_{n}}
.

Like substitution, our new admissible rules are definable purely on
well-scoped syntax.

Definition A.1
.

A
scope
is an ordered list of variable names and lock names,
where the variable names have no associated types. Unlike ordinary
type theory, the relative position of variables and locks is
important. Every ordinary context and telescope has an underlying
scope.

The judgement
G\vdash r\,\,\mathsf{rawterm}
denotes a raw term in scope

G
. These are defined as usual, but importantly, in a well-scoped
term, all variable uses have the correct number of associated keys
for their position in the scope.

Definition A.2
.

The unit and counit operations

\frac{G,G^{\prime\prime}\vdash r\,\,\mathsf{rawterm}G,G^{\prime}\vdash t\,\,\mathsf{rawterm}\text{ for }\mathcal{L}\in\mathsf{locks}(G^{\prime})}{G,G^{\prime},G^{\prime\prime}\vdash r[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{rawterm}}\quad\textsc{left=counit,fraction={-{\,-\,}-}}\frac{G,i,\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}},G^{\prime}\vdash r\,\,\mathsf{rawterm}}{G,G^{\prime}\vdash r[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{rawterm}}\quad\textsc{left=unit,fraction={-{\,-\,}-}}

are defined on raw syntax as described in
Section

2
.

These operations on raw syntax satisfy a number of associativity
properties whose mind-numbing proofs we delay until later.

Proposition A.3
.

The counit operation is admissible on context telescopes, types and terms.

\frac{\Gamma\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\,\,\mathsf{tele}\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\mathbb{T}\text{ for }\mathcal{L}\in\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime})}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{tele}}\quad\textsc{left=counit-tele,fraction={-{\,-\,}-}}\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash a:A\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash t:\mathbb{T}\text{ for }\mathcal{L}\in\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime})}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash a[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]}\quad\textsc{left=counit,fraction={-{\,-\,}-}}

Proof.

On telescopes:

Case:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\,\,\mathsf{tele}\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash A\,\,\mathsf{type}}{\Gamma\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A\,\,\mathsf{tele}}

By induction
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{tele}
and
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{type}
, so

\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{tele}

Case:

\frac{\Gamma\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\,\,\mathsf{tele}}{\Gamma\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}\,\,\mathsf{tele}}

By induction
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{tele}
so
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime}\vdash\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{L}}\,\,\mathsf{tele}
.

On terms:

Case:

\frac{\Gamma_{1},x:A,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash s:\mathbb{T}\text{ for }\mathcal{J}\in\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma_{2})\quad\quad\Gamma_{1},x:A,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash s^{\prime\prime}:\mathbb{T}\text{ for }\mathcal{K}\in\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime})}{\Gamma_{1},x:A,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash x\llbracket\vec{s}/{\mathcal{J}},\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket:A[\vec{s}/{\mathcal{J}},\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}/{\mathcal{K}}]}

Inductively, we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},x:A,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash\vec{s}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]:\mathbb{T}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},x:A,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]:\mathbb{T}

Also inductively, we may use the latter to form

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},x:A,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash\vec{t}[\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}]:\mathbb{T}

These are all the keys required to form

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1},x:A,\Gamma_{2},\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]

\displaystyle\vdash x\llbracket\vec{s}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{J}},\vec{t}[\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}],\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket

\displaystyle:A[\vec{s}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{J}},\vec{t}[\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}],\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}]

And finally
A[\vec{s}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{J}},\vec{t}[\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}]/{\mathcal{L}},\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}]\equiv A[\vec{s}/{\mathcal{J}},\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}/{\mathcal{K}}][\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]
by
Lemma

A.5
.

Case:

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1},x:A,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{2}\vdash s^{\prime\prime}:\mathbb{T}\text{ for }\mathcal{K}\in\mathsf{locks}(\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{2})}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1},x:A,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{2}\vdash x\llbracket\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket:A[\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}/{\mathcal{K}}]}

Inductively
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],x:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{2}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash s^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]:\mathbb{T}
, and we may form
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{1}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],x:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],\Gamma^{\prime\prime}_{2}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash x\llbracket\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}\rrbracket:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}][\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}]
. Finally,
A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}][\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]/{\mathcal{K}}]\equiv A[\vec{s^{\prime\prime}}/{\mathcal{K}}][\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]
by
Lemma

A.6
.

Case:

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash A\,\,\mathsf{type}\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime},x:A\vdash B\,\,\mathsf{type}}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(x:A)}}}{\prod_{(x:A)}}{\prod_{(x:A)}}{\prod_{(x:A)}}B\,\,\mathsf{type}}

Inductively
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{type}
and also, with the telescope
\Gamma^{\prime\prime}
extended,
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],x:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash B[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{type}
, and we may form
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash\mathchoice{{\textstyle\prod_{(x:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}])}}}{\prod_{(x:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}])}}{\prod_{(x:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}])}}{\prod_{(x:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}])}}B[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{type}
.

Case:

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}\vdash A\,\,\mathsf{type}}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}A\,\,\mathsf{type}}

Inductively
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}\vdash A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\,\,\mathsf{type}
and so we may form
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\vdash\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}(A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}])\,\,\mathsf{type}
.

Case:

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime},\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}\vdash a:A}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}.a:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}A}

Similar: Inductively
\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime\prime}[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}],\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}\vdash a[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]:A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]

.

Case:

\frac{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime},i:\mathbb{T}\vdash r:\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}A}{\Gamma,\Gamma^{\prime\prime}\vdash r(\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i.):A[\raisebox{-0.5pt}{\lx@scalerel@obj{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin={c}]{185.0}{$\lambda$}}}}{}i./\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.8}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.5}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}{\scalebox{0.4}{\text{\faIconFromMacro{faLock}}}}_{\mathcal{K}}]\,\,\mathsf{type}}

Inductively

. By definition
(\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}A)[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}]\equiv\ThisStyle{\raisebox{0.70007pt}{$\SavedStyle\surd_{\hskip-2.04861pt\mathcal{K}}$}}(A[\vec{t}/{\mathcal{L}}])
